<table><tr><td>ReqID</td><td>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td>102.4.1</td><td>The IMA safety application shall warn drivers about DSRC-equipped vehicles that cross the intersection</td></tr></table>

## 3.8.2.6 Vehicle Turning Right in Front of a Transit Vehicle

<table><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">ReqID</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.5.4</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The VTRW application shall detect whether a vehicle is turning right in front of transitvehicle via the position and heading data received in a basic safety message (from aremote vehicle) based on the PVT accuracy for positioning.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.5.5</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The application alert mode in the VTRW application shall be active when the transitvehicle stops within a bus stop geographic zone.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.5.6</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The application alert mode shall terminate when the transit vehicle stops within a busstop geographic zone.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.5.7</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The VTRW application shall only issue alerts to the driver when the transit vehicleindicates an impending crash.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.5.8</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">A VTRW application shall advise if a remote vehicle which originates directly behindthe transit vehicle at a bus stop begins to pass to the left of the transit vehicle as thetransit vehicle is departing a bus stop.</td></tr><tr><td>102.5.9</td><td>A VTRW alert shall alert the driver if a remote vehicle which originates directly behind the transit vehicle at a bus stop passes to the left of the transit vehicle as the transit vehicle is departing a bus stop and the remote vehicle's position and heading indicates an intent to return to or cross the lane of the transit vehicle.</td></tr><tr><td>102.5.17</td><td>The VTRW application shall present the alerts regardless of traffic signal status.</td></tr><tr><td>102.5.26</td><td>The VTRW application shall have access to the transit vehicle's gear position.</td></tr></table>

## 3.8.3 V2I Application

## 3.8.3.1 Speed Compliance

<table><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>ReqID</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>101.1.2</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The SPDCOMP application shall obtain regulatory speed limits from the MAPmessage per SAE J2735-201603 based on the host vehicle&#x27;s current location.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>101.1.4</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The SPDCOMP application shall trigger an alert when either the vehicle speedexceeds the recommended speed by a configured amount or for a configured periodof time by time of day. (Note: configurable speed (Excessive Speed AmountThreshold), configurable time (Excessive Speed Time) are defined in Table E-1 andtime of day is defined in Table D-1 in the Appendix).</td></tr></table>

3.8.3.2 Curve Speed Compliance
<table><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>ReqID</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>101.2.10</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The CSPD-COMP application shall determine if there is a curve with reduced speedahead.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>101.2.11</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The CSPD-COMP application shall advise the driver of a curve with reduced speedahead.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>101.2.14</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The CSPD-COMP application shall determine the difference between the postedspeed on the upcoming curve and the vehicle&#x27;s current speed.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>101.2.15</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>If the vehicle speed is greater than the posted curve speed, the Vehicle CSPD-COMP Application shall advise the driver in time for the driver to reduce vehiclespeed to the posted speed limit before the vehicle enters the curve.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>101.2.16</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>While the vehicle is in the curve, the CSPD-COMP application shall trigger a driveralert when the host vehicle speed exceeds the posted speed plus the ExcessiveCurve Speed Amount Threshold for a time period exceeding the Excessive CurveSpeed Time Threshold.</td></tr></table>

3.8.3.3 Speed Compliance / Work Zone
<table><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>ReqID</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>101.3.9</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The SPDCOMPWZ application shall acquire the following from Speed Zoneinformation for use in SPDCOMPWZ advisory and alert calculations:1. Posted Reduced Speed Zone Speed Limit2.Work Zone geometric information</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>101.3.10</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The SPDCOMPWZ application shall determine if there is a reduced speed zoneahead.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>101.3.11</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The SPDCOMPWZ application shall advise the driver of a reduced speed zoneahead.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>101.3.13</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The SPDCOMPWZ application shall determine the speed of the host vehicle basedon accuracy and (configurable) threshold per J2945/1.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>101.3.14</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The SPDCOMPWZ application shall determine the difference between the postedspeed on the upcoming reduced speed zone and the vehicle&#x27;s current speed.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>101.3.15</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>If the vehicle speed is greater than the reduced speed zone, the SPDCOMPWZapplication shall advise the driver in time for the driver to reduce vehicle speed to theposted speed limit before the vehicle enters the zone.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>101.3.16</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The SPDCOMPWZ application shall determine if the vehicle exceeds the Work ZonePosted Speed plus the Excessive Zone Speed Amount Threshold, for a periodexceeding the Excessive Zone Speed Time Threshold.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>101.3.17</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>While the vehicle is in the speed zone, the SPDCOMPWZ application shall trigger adriver alert when the vehicle speed exceeds the Work Zone Posted Speed plus theExcessive Zone Speed Amount Threshold, for a period exceeding the ExcessiveZone Speed Time Threshold.</td></tr></table>

3.8.3.4 Red Light Violation Warning
<table><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">ReqID</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.6.1</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The RLVW Application shall receive the SPaT and MAP data sent by the RSUs to thevehicles deployed along the NYC CVPD corridors as per J2735.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.6.2</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The RLVW application shall use the same time source as infrastructure SPaTapplications.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.6.9</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The RLVW application shall acquire SPaT information, through the Host VehicleASD, from the intersection RSU and signal controller at a signalized intersectionwithin RSU's DSRC range and in the vehicle's direction of travel.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.6.10</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The RLVW application shall acquire the location of the stop bars at signalizedintersections at which RLVW is operating from the intersection MAP Message.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.6.11</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The RLVW application shall acquire the following from Intersection SPaT messagesfor use in RLVW advisory and alert calculations:1.Intersection geometric information2.Signal Phase and Timing information of the traffic signal at the Intersection3.Intersection status of the traffic controller4.  Traffic law restrictions for the intersection</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.6.12</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The Vehicle RLVW Application shall be inactive if one or more of the followingconditions occur:1. Vehicle position and accuracy information is not available2. Vehicle Speed is not available3.Communication failure between Vehicle and Infrastructure4.SPaT information is not available5.MAP information is not available6.Vehicle Heading information is not available</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.6.13</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The RLVW application shall calculate if a vehicle will violate the Red Light stop bar ata signalized intersection based on its current speed, heading, acceleration, location,location of stop bars, stop bar tolerance and yellow duration tolerance.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.6.14</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The RLVW application shall trigger a driver alert for a vehicle approaching asignalized intersection when it determines that a stop is required and the vehicle willviolate the Red Light stop bar at a signalized intersection based on its current speed,heading, acceleration, location and the location of stop bars.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.6.15</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The RLVW application shall alert drivers in time for the driver to take action for arequired stop at a signalized intersection.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.6.16</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The RLVW Application shall not alert the driver if the vehicle has safely stopped atthe intersection and subsequently accelerates such that a violation occurs.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.6.18</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The RLVW application shall acquire the following from Intersection MAP messagesfor use in RLVW advisory and alert calculations:1.Intersection geometric information2.Signal Phase and Timing information of the traffic signal at the Intersection3.Intersection status of the traffic controller4.Traffic law restrictions for the intersection</td></tr></table>

3.8.3.5 Oversize Vehicle Compliance
<table><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">ReqID</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">103.1.1</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The ASD shall advise the driver of a potential crash before the bridge, overpass, ortunnel to exit the restricted roadway and find an alternate route. (Note: AdvisoryDistance Threshold Parameter is defined in Table E-1 in the Appendix.)</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">103.1.2</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The ASD shall warn the driver of an impending crash before the over-height bridge,overpass, or tunnel to stop the vehicle completely and avoid the crash. (Note:Warning Distance Threshold Parameter is defined in Table E-1 in the Appendix.)</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">103.2.1</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The OVC application shall calculate whether or not the vehicle is at risk of crash bycomparing the vehicle's height and the height of the roadway restriction.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">103.3.1</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The OVC application shall utilize the host vehicle's pre-established height indetermining whether to alert the driver.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">103.3.2</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The OVC application shall determine the location of the vehicle relative to thelocation of the size-restricted or weight restricted roadway.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">103.3.3</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The RSU shall contain the roadway's clearance height and communicate it to theASD via DSRC.</td></tr></table>

## 3.8.3.6 Emergency Communications and Evacuation Information

<table><tr><td>ReqID</td><td>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td>105.1.2</td><td>The NYC CVPD performance monitoring subsystem shall utilize the post-processed event data and measure the number of vehicles receiving emergency information Before and After periods.</td></tr></table>

## 3.8.3.7 Pedestrian in Signalized Intersection Warning

<table><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>ReqID</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>104.1.5</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>A PEDINXWALK alert shall be displayed if a pedestrian has been detected in acrosswalk that intersects the Transit Vehicle&#x27;s planned forward, left or right turn routeat the intersection.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>104.1.7</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The Pedestrian Detector shall send a pedestrian detected signal when a pedestrianis present in the crosswalk it monitors.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>104.1.8</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The Pedestrian Detectors shall be pole mountable.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>104.1.10</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The PEDINXWALK System Latency for pedestrian detection shall be no more than 2seconds from detecting pedestrian to alert.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>104.1.12</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The PEDINXWALK application shall receive Signal Phase and Timing for PedestrianLanes and Pedestrian presence detection from the SPaT system.</td></tr></table>

## 3.8.4 I2P Application

## 3.8.4.1 Mobile Accessible Pedestrian Signal System

<table><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>ReqID</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>104.2.3</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The PED-SIG application shall correctly discern the pedestrian&#x27;s position andintended crossing direction.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>104.2.4</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The PED-SIG application shall provide status of the walk signal in the intendeddirection.</td></tr></table>

U.S. Department of Transportation Office of the Assistant Secretary for Research and Technology Intelligent Transportation System Joint Program Office

<table><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">ReqID</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.2.7</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PED-SIG application shall advise (via audio) the pedestrian how to use theapplication.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.3.1</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PED-SIG application shall receive intersection geometry information from theRSU based on J2735-201603.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.3.2</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PED-SIG application shall use the intersection geometry information todetermine the pedestrian's orientation.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.3.3</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PED-SIG application shall use the intersection geometry information provided bythe MAP message to determine the crosswalk geometry of the intended crossing.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.3.8</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PED-SIG Application shall determine the intended crossing time from theintersection geographic information (distance) and the expected pedestrian travelrate (configurable).</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.3.9</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PED-SIG application shall collect data on the map error status notification.(partial, unable to detect). (Note: this is intended to assist the system introubleshooting problems; the quality of the MAP data is critical to the application andthis requirements ensures that such issues are made known to the central system forcorrection. This assumes that the application notifies the PED whenever itencounters a geographic computation error.)</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.4.6</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PED-SIG application on the PID shall acquire credentials from the SCMS thatwill allow it to authenticate the SPaT and MAP messages received.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.4.8</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The SCMS shall be able to set service specific permissions associated with thefuture support of pedestrian requests from the PID.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.4.10</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Applications on the PID shall be able to sign messages transmitted to the RSU.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.4.11</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Applications shall be capable of including a unique ID which shall remain stable(unchanged) throughout the PID's interaction with a single intersection.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.5.1</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PED-SIG application shall provide information to the pedestrian based on thecontent of the SPaT and MAP message at the nearest intersection.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.5.2</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The Application on the PID shall assist the pedestrian in determining his/herorientation. (Note: Definition of orientation: to understand which street crossing thepedestrian is facing.)</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.5.3</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PID application shall provide information to the pedestrian regarding theavailability of PED support services at the intersection. (If there is an RSU)</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.5.4</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Application shall provide information to the pedestrian indicating the status of thepedestrian signal for the intended crossing including walk time remaining, clearancetime remaining, and time until the next walk signal is expected.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.5.6</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PED Applications shall allow the pedestrian to configure selected parametersand characteristics such as sound volume, voice commands, and walking speeds.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.5.8</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The application shall notify the pedestrian if there is a preemption or priority change(TSP) operation taking place which may disrupt the signal timing.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.5.9</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PED application shall collect DSRC RF receive levels. (Note: this is for analysisof the reliability of the RSU to PED messages and the PED to RSU messages.)</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.5.10</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The Application shall calculate intersection crossing information based onpedestrian's origin, destination, departure range, arrival range. (Note: this is forcoordination of signal timing phase.)</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.5.11</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PED-SIG Application shall collect the performance data listed in Table G-3.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.5.13</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The Application shall collect metadata that includes the date and time when systeminformation was generated.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.5.14</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PED-SIG application shall receive signal timing and controller status from theRSU through the SPaT message.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.5.18</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Every PID shall be able to change signing keys per SAE J2945/9 Section 6.6. (Note:the signing will be static during interactions with a single intersection.)</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.6.2</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PED-SIG application shall continuously notify the user that it is operating.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.6.3</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PED-SIG application shall be able to provide requested information within 10seconds.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.6.3.1</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">If the delay will be longer than 10 seconds, then the PED-SIG application shallprovide information indicating how long it is expected to complete the operation.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.6.4</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The applications shall be able to measure and log the time required to provide thepedestrian the information.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.6.11</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PID device shall be able to function in a communication-saturated environment.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.8.4</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PID system shall ensure geo-location information is used for PED-SIGapplication only.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.8.9</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">All data transmitted between the PID and the NYU server shall be encrypted toprotect any personal information.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.8.10</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Data stored by the NYU server or exported for independent evaluation shall beprocessed to remove any personal information and any attributes including exactlocation and exact time such that the data cannot be associated with a particularperson or event.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">104.9.4</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The PID shall use the channel assignments defined in Table F-1.</td></tr></table>

## Chapter 4. System Interfaces

This section contains the interface requirements of the NYC CVPD components and their external capabilities.

## 4.1 Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS)

<table><tr><td>ReqID</td><td>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td>401.17.1</td><td>Each DSRC device shall obtain its time and position from the GNSS per the requirements of J2945/1 Section 6.2.</td></tr></table>

## 4.2 Location Correction

## 4.2.1 Triangulation for ASD Location Accuracy

<table><tr><td>ReqID</td><td>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td>401.15.3</td><td>RSUs shall exceed 802.11 ACK requirements in the following manner: Antenna referenced ACK turnaround time must be in (SIFS-12.5, SIFS+12.5) ns 95% for cable tested non-CSD signals. Note that any RSU employing the NXP based SAF5200 will support this by default. The position provided by the WSA shall be provided by the central system based on the 3D surveyed position.</td></tr></table>

## 4.3 Network Time Reference

<table><tr><td>ReqID</td><td>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td>401.16.1</td><td>Devices unable to receive timing information per J2945/1 Section 6.2 shall set their time from an authenticated time reference using the Network Time Protocol Version 4 per Internet Engineering Task Force RFC 5905-5908.</td></tr></table>

## 4.4 Security Credential Management System (SCMS)

Note that other security requirements are listed in Section 3.3 System Security.

<table><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>ReqID</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>401.2.1</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The device supplier shall provide devices provisioned with valid enrollmentcertificates.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>401.2.2</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The device supplier shall provide devices that meet the interface requirements of theUSDOT&#x27;s certification program.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>401.6.3</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The SCMS shall maintain the blacklist internally.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>401.6.4</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The back office system shall acquire one SCMS certificate from each installed DSRCdevice and associate this certificate with the installed DSRC device. (Note: thisrequirement is temporary and will become obsolete after the SCMS deploysmisbehavior detection and reporting features.)</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>401.6.5</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The back office system administrator shall provide a DSRC device&#x27;s acquired SCMScertificate when requesting that the SCMS registration authority add the associatedDSRC device to the Certificate Revocation List (CRL). (Note: this requirement istemporary and will become obsolete after the SCMS deploys misbehavior detectionand reporting features.)</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>401.14.21</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Devices shall implement certificate download per the SCMS Interface (detailedrequirements to be derived during Phase 2 as the final interface document is not yetpublished)</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>403.1.1</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The device supplier shall sign the firmware images and manage the certificatemanagement process for the firmware images.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>403.1.1.1</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The SCMS certificate shall have a lifespan of 3 years instead of weeks.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>403.1.1.2</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The SCMS signature scheme shall provide at least 128-bit security.</td></tr></table>

## 4.5 Object Registration and Discovery Service (ORDS)

As stated in Chapter 2, the ORDS is expected to be developed and provided by the USDOT. Its requirements will be detailed in the design phase.

## 4.6 Data Distribution System (DDS)

As stated in Section 2.1, the ODE has replaced the Data Distribution System (DDS) as the platform for routing the data from multiple data sources to a common, integrated format for subscribers to software applications such as CV applications. However, at this time the USDOT ODE is not expected to play a role and interact directly with the NYC CVPD system. Its requirements will be detailed in the design phase.

## 4.7 Research Data Exchange (RDE)

<table><tr><td>ReqID</td><td>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td>510.11.1</td><td>The interface for transferring performance measurement information to the USDOT Research Data Exchange (RDE) shall be negotiated by the NYC CVPD project team and the USDOT RDE operators during the detailed design of the system in Phase 2.</td></tr></table>

## 4.8 National Weather Service

<table><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>ReqID</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>510.5.1</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The NYC CVPD performance monitoring subsystem shall collect hourly weather dataobservations from the following National Weather Service (NWS) stations in NYC:Central Park (KNYC)Kennedy International Airport (KJFK)LaGuardia Airport (KLGA)</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>510.5.1.1</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The NYC CVPD performance monitoring subsystem shall obtain the weather datalisted in Table G-2.</td></tr></table>

## 4.9 Foreign Dedicated Short Range Communications Devices

<table><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>ReqID</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>110.4.1</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Foreign light-duty vehicles shall transmit DSRC BSM messages in accordance withthe J2945/1 standard.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>110.4.2</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Foreign trucks shall transmit DSRC BSM messages in accordance with the J2945/1and shall contain the following Part Il SupplementalVehicleExtensions objects:classification, and vehicleData (height and mass) as defined in J2735 Section 6.133</td></tr></table>

## 4.10 Vehicle Data Bus

<table><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">ReqID</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.5.27</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The ASD shall have access to the host vehicle's brake status.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">202.4.1</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The ASD shall monitor the items in Table E-5 on the in-vehicle network.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">203.12.1</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The ASD shall utilize the OBD-Il port, J-bus, or CAN bus.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">203.12.2</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The ASD shall conform to the associated SAE interface requirements for OBD-Il port,J-bus, or CAN bus. (Clarification: the intent of this requirement is to prevent the ASDfrom interfering with other in-vehicle systems.)</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">203.12.3</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The application in the ASD shall collect and process vehicle-related informationthrough a direct connection or a splitter cable. (Note: individual application dataneeds will be addressed through each application's requirement.)</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">203.25.1</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The ASD interface to the CAN bus shall not degrade or interfere with vehicle's normaloperation.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">203.25.2</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The ASD's interface to the CAN bus shall not interfere with the vehicle's passenger'ssafety systems (e.g., restraints and extrication).</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">401.3.6</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The ASD shall carry out plausibility checking on the internal CAN/J-Bus data.</td></tr></table>

## 4.11 Advanced Traffic Signal Controllers

<table><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>ReqID</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>405.3.1</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Advanced Traffic Signal Controllers shall use TAl time to issue security credentials.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>405.3.2</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Advanced Traffic Signal Controllers shall issue messages to DSRC devices withsecurity credentials that meet this document&#x27;s IEEE 1609.2 requirements.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>405.3.3</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Advanced Traffic Signal Controllers shall maintain an authenticated NTP based timereference.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>405.3.4</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Advanced Traffic Signal Controllers shall export their UTC times (Line Frequencyreferenced from the traffic signal system) as UTC times referenced from theirauthenticated NTP based time reference.</td></tr></table>

## 4.12 Traffic Signal System

<table><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>ReqID</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>Requirement Text</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>405.4.1</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The CV system shall export RSU status to the traffic signal system for display on thetraffic signal system map.</td></tr><tr><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>405.4.2</td><td rowspan=1 colspan=1>The CV system shall export RSU RF signal range information to the traffic signalsystem for display on the traffic signal system map.</td></tr></table>

## APPENDIX D Definitions

Table D-1 below lists the definitions of key terms in the system requirements.

Table D-1. Definitions
<table><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">ReqID</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Term</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Description</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">101.1.4</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Time of day</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">NYC CVPD system operating hours</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">101.2.11,101.3.11</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Ahead</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">See MUTCD Table 2C-4</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">101.2.15</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Curve alert distance(feet)</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Distance required for a vehicle to decelerate from its current speed to the curve advisory speed at adeceleration rate [that is configurable by vehicle type], plus an allowance of &lt;TBD&gt; seconds for driverperception time (Note: this will be detailed in the design phase.)</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">101.2.16</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Warning</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Notification from the ASD to the driver once the ASD determines that the application threshold has beenexceeded and violation or crash is imminent.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">101.2.16</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Alert</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Trigger generated by the once the ASD determines that the application threshold has been exceeded. Oncethe alert is triggered, the ASD generates the corresponding warning to the driver.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">101.2.16</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Time period</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Amount of time detected by the ASD in which the application threshold is exceeded by the vehicle</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">101.2.6,102.6.7</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Sufficiently accurateposition</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Road-level accuracy (which needs to be defined at the system level for use in other apps)</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">101.2.6,102.6.7</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Sufficiently accuratespeed (mph)</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Calculation of where to begin the alert use the lower end of the 90% confidence interval for speed. That waythe app will issue an alert only if it is confident that one is warranted.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">101.2.6,102.6.7</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Sufficiently accurateheading</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">First, confidence of the direction of travel on the road (again, needs a system-level determination). Second,projected path includes an instrumented curve. Ordinarily, that will be clear to project. When an exit is withinrange, we do not know whether the exit will be taken. So say that we know the heading only when the exitand the road have instrumented curves.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">101.3.11,401.14.23</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Advise, advisory</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Initial notification from the ASD to the driver once the ASD determines that the application threshold willexceed and violation or crash is likely to occur.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.5.5,102.5,6</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Geographic zone</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Pre-defined radius around a point of interest (e.g. bus stop) stored in the MAP message.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">ReqlD</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Term</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Description</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.5.6</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Application alert mode</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The control mechanism for audible notifications in either Silent or Active mode, with the default being theActive mode.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.5.7,103.1.2</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Impending crash</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Crash that is imminent if the driver does not take action after receiving warning from the ASD.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">102.6.15</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">In time</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Time spent by the driver for processing the application alert from the ASD and taking action (e.g. slow down,divert to an alternate route).</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">202.1.3,501.3.6</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Externally</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Parameter download process from the TMC to the applications in the ASD.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">202.9.2</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Sudden change ofdirection or speed,sudden stop, orimpact</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Road-level change in acceleration measured by the accelerometer in the ASD. (Note: this will be detailed inthe design phase.)</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">203.22.1,</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Externally</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">CV equipment installation in the vehicle's exterior.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">404.3.1</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Nearly simultaneously</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Alerts from multiple applications that occur almost at the same time by the same ASD.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">404.3.1</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Threat arbitration rules</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Mechanism established by recording parameters for controlling simultaneous or nearly simultaneous alertsfrom multiple applications and preventing multiple applications from warning the driver, based on priorityamong the alerts as listed in Table E-2.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">501.2.1.1</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Rotating 5 minute logof raw data</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Note: the event log entries are expected to be recorded at the frequency of the available data or 10 Hz,whichever is less.For Example, SPaT messages may occur at 10 Hz while MAP messages may occur at 2 Hz, thus the numberof each type will vary.Clarification: Measurements shall be recorded whenever they change or at 1 second intervals, whichever isshorter. Each message received (BSM, SPaT, MAP, TIM) shall be recorded regardless of whether the datachanges or not as described below.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">501.2.1.3</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Log entry</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Collection of stored event logs in the ASDs</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">501.4.2</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Event record</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Collection of BSMs generated by the ASDs before and after the alert is triggered.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">503.1.2</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Speed variability</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">The range of vehicle speeds (e.g. 25th to 75th percentile) for each vehicle fleet on a given roadway segmentfor a given time period from the Before period to the After period.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">ReqID</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Term</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Description</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">510.10.1</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Active mode</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Operational state in which the device transmits and/or receives DSRC messages while event records arecollected.</td></tr><tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">510.10.1</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Silent mode</td><td colspan="1" rowspan="1">Operational state in which the device transmits and/or receives DSRC messages but no event records arecollected.</td></tr></table>

Table E-2 below lists the parameters needed by multiple safety applications. All applications need a parameter that can be used to stop the application from providing alerts. For most applications, this can be the minimum speed where the application is active. If the minimum speed for an application is set to 100 mph, the application is essentially disabled. The oversized vehicle compliance application does not have a minimum speed, but it can be disabled by broadcasting an expiration time for the Traveler Information Message (TIM) it is based on. The Emergency Communications and Evacuation Information (EVAC) app operates via TIM messages as well, and is essentially always inactive unless an EVAC TIM message has been received.

All vehicle safety applications also have a recorded sound that plays to present the alert to the driver. Audible messages for the Emergency Evacuation application are transmitted as needed, so the ability to download a sound is required for this application. Using that ability to change the alert sounds for other applications during the testing period will provide flexibility to experiment with buzzers and phrases.

A minimum confidence level parameter will enable the applications to be adjusted for accuracy. Exactly how that works will depend on the implementation.

Vehicle-specific values, notably size, are encoded in the ASD when it is installed in the vehicle. Whether these should be adjustable only with physica access to the vehicle is a question for discussion. Locating a vehicle known to have incorrect dimensions could be difficult. On the other hand, dimensions are best confirmed when the vehicle is present.

The vehicle-dependent maximum braking capability is a remotely tunable parameter needed by some applications. The vehicles do not sense whether they are loaded, so the braking capability will assume the vehicle is loaded. None of the applications account for surface conditions or other weatherrelated conditions

To support the performance evaluation, engineering data will be recorded whenever an application is triggered. Event recording parameters are the pretrigger recording time and the post-trigger recording time, which may vary by application. Additional event recording parameters, such as recording resolution (e.g., percentage of BSMs to be recorded), may also be desired.

The top-level software on the ASD needs parameters to utilize threat arbitration and control simultaneous or nearly simultaneous alerts from multiple applications. The priority among the alerts is most simply stored as an order, 1-13, of the vehicle safety applications. There is also a minimum quiet time between two alerts, or alert spacing.

Note that Table E-2 provides an initial list of the parameters in the NYC CVPD system. Therefore, each application includes a number of free parameters to be considered for future decisions.

# As-Built Addendum

This section highlights changes made to the overall SyRS due to the final deployment and the schedule of activity. Elements within this section supersede portions of the document not altered to reflect the details of the changes.

a. The PID application to assist the visually impaired pedestrian at CV equipped intersections has changed significantly. The SPaT information is received from the ASTC, combined with the MAP information stored at the TMC and sent to the AWS cloud where the SmartCross application process the data and distributes it to the appropriate PID using a cellular service. The data is then collected and exported to the NYU data collection system where it is evaluated. Instead of distributing PIDs to the user community for testing/trials and evaluation, the 10 prototypes will be used with selected participants who will test/trial the PID application in a protected and assisted environment. Evaluation of the preliminary results indicated that the application was not robust enough to be distributed without supervision in a protected environment. The detailed design is provided in a separate document. Section 2.3.4 and Figure 2-6 have been updated accordingly. In addition, the PID will not be enrolled with the SCMS as previously described.

b. Note that the communications between the TMC and the RSU uses 2 different protocols: SNMPv3 for management of the MIB objects supported by the RSU, and XFER a proprietary protocol developed by the RSU vendor for uploading and downloading files used for data collection, and RSU configuration. It should be noted that NTCIP 1218 had not been developed at the time of this project, and it was necessary to utilize the vendor’s protocol to support these exchanges. The vendor has made this protocol readily and publicly available without restriction for use with their RSUs.

c. While there is some discussion of the use of UPS participation and the participation of the Taxi fleets/owners, both of these did not become active participants in the project, The fleet is primarily composed of various City fleets including DCAS, Parks, DOT, Department of Corrections (DOC), Department of Environmental Protection (DEP), Department of Health Services (DHS), and other service fleets operated by NYC. The City fleets are generally equipped with Geotab devices which monitors their general usage; this data has been available to the CVPD project to determine the general routes and locations frequented by the City fleets participating in the CVPD project. This data will be used to evaluate the accuracy of the data collected and to assist the project in monitoring the health of the CV equipped vehicles.

d. The HSM is a rack-mountable appliance installed at the TMC to expand TMC operations with direct-to-vehicle messaging over latest V2X network technology. Traveler Information, MAP, and other infrastructure messages are inspected and digitally signed to prevent hacking so TMC messages can be trusted. The HSM authenticates incoming messages and signs certificates for outgoing messages to and from the TMC. The HSM inspects and formats TMC messages for acceptance by V2X networked devices. Installed RSUs and ASDs do not require any additional components. In addition to the NYCDOT TMC back-office management functions, the HSM has been critical for maintaining the security of the data being transmitted. Also known as the TMC Authority, provides complete support for IEEE 1609.2 algorithms and protocols including SAE J2735, and J2945/x. It also provides FIPS 140-2 Level 3 protection for V2X signing keys. The TMC Authority checks and digitally signs traffic management center messages for instant verification and acceptance, compliant with IEEE 1609.2 standards and cryptography. Data is

U.S. Department of Transportation Office of the Assistant Secretary for Research and Technology Intelligent Transportation System Joint Program Office

secured inside and out with FIPS 140-2 Level 3 protection of keys and TLS v1.2 tunnels to TMC servers. High availability network failover is standard, including redundant power supplies and storage; the TMC Authority maintains trusted reliable operation, even in untrusted environments. e. Achieving location augmentation has been critical especially in the urban canyon parts of the NYC CV Pilot area. To improve location accuracy for the DSRC devices in the NYC CV project, using the DSRC Radio Technical Commission for Maritime (RTCM) was considered first. As defined in SAE J2735, the RTCM messages would be transmitted along with the other DSRC messages over channel 172. However, the V2XLocate technology was chosen instead after multiple tests and demonstrations in the Manhattan grid section of the NYC CV pilot area.
